[1] The Hacker News - Hackers Compromise 24 Check Point Management Servers in Suspected AI-Driven Attack Campaign
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Wed, 25 Nov 2026 12:20:19 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/hackers-compromise-24-check-point.html

[TAM METİN - 607 kelime]
An unknown threat actor has been using artificial intelligence (AI)-driven offensive tooling in a campaign targeting Check Point Security Management servers, impacting 22 distinct organizations across the world.

The intrusion activity, tracked by GreyNoise under the moniker GreyNoise Threat 13 (GT13), is assessed to have targeted organizations in the financial services, energy, government, transportation, mining, engineering, and manufacturing sectors across the U.K., Ireland, France, Netherlands, Greece, Germany, Austria, Bulgaria, Poland, India, Thailand, Vietnam, South Korea, Hong Kong, China, Australia, and the U.S.

Confirmed attack activity has been recorded between October 17 and November 2, 2026, with the threat actor using 99 IP addresses to conduct scanning, exploitation, and control. Most of these IP addresses are associated with disposable network infrastructure.

The campaign has not been attributed to any known threat actor or group. It's suspected that the attackers are relying on agentic harnesses and systems for vulnerability scanning, exploitation, and automatic interaction with compromised assets.

The threat actor has also been observed using tools like Nmap, Chisel, Impacket, Paramiko, and other Python scripts to carry out internal reconnaissance, privilege escalation, and lateral movement on victim environments.

"We witnessed the GT13 actor use 99 IP addresses, all of which were either egresses through ExpressVPN or compromised victim Check Point devices, including activity over 1400+ network sessions, 800+ command line artifacts, and 1200+ files downloaded, uploaded, or created," GreyNoise said.

In one case, the attackers have been found using an automated scanning and exploitation pipeline to compromise an HFS server using CVE-2026-61500, a critical flaw in Rejetto HFS, in early October 2026 to obtain control of the server and issue commands, including adding an SSH key to the "/root/.ssh/authorized_keys" file.

Subsequently, GT13 is said to have used the compromised server as a relay for follow-on operations centered around saving command results and exfiltrating data from 24 previously compromised Check Point Security Management servers. The attackers also unsuccessfully attempted to deploy a Linux implant named ZULT to the compromised Rejetto HFS server.

"The GT13 adversary had access to the victim Check Point Security Management Servers before they compromised the HFS server," the cybersecurity company said. "We could not determine when or how the attacker gained access to those Check Point assets."

The attackers also moved to burrow deeper into one of the victim networks, using a script for a now-patched Check Point flaw (CVE-2026-16232) to launch over 100 management tasks on multiple Check Point Management Servers to -

Steal firewall databases and internal certificate authority files

Mint a new certificate

Unload security policies, effectively disabling firewall protections

Conduct internal network reconnaissance and attempt lateral movement

The post-compromise activity also involved attempts to target other enterprise systems, such as Citrix NetScaler (CVE-2023-4966), Ivanti Connect Secure (CVE-2024-21893), VMware vCenter (CVE-2021-21972 and CVE-2023-34048), Microsoft Exchange (CVE-2021-26855 and CVE-2021-34473), and Microsoft Netlogon (CVE-2020-1472).

In tandem, the threat actor has attempted to exploit various other security flaws, including, but not limited to -

CVE-2024-21893 (Ivanti Connect Secure)

CVE-2025-5777 (Citrix NetScaler)

CVE-2026-41089 (Windows Netlogon)

CVE-2026-98432 (Linux IPv4 UAF)

GreyNoise has theorized that GT13 has in its possession a "mature AI-enabled offensive apparatus" that it uses to weaponize vulnerabilities, scan for susceptible assets, and exploit them to gain access to systems that can be later used as operational relays or true targets.

"The activity reported by the GreyNoise article involves CVE-2026-16232, a previously disclosed vulnerability with patches already available, and does not represent a new vulnerability or exploitation technique," Sergey Shykevich, Director, Threat Intelligence at Check Point Software Technologies, told The Hacker News.

"Our priority remains ensuring customers apply the available updates and follow Check Point’s published hardening guidance to restrict management access and identify suspicious activity."

[2] The Hacker News - Com-Affiliated Hackers Hijack Cloud Accounts via Vishing for Data Theft and Extortion
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Wed, 25 Nov 2026 12:07:06 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/com-affiliated-hackers-hijack-cloud.html

[TAM METİN - 902 kelime]
Palo Alto Networks Unit 42 has disclosed that it's tracking and has responded to "numerous waves of vishing attacks" orchestrated by Com-affiliated threat actors since August 2026 that have led to data theft and extortion attempts.

The attacks have targeted financial services, healthcare, and legal services located in the U.S., the U.K., and Australia. Other targets include construction, high-technology, transportation, manufacturing, pharmaceuticals, insurance, and real estate sectors.

"The victims share a common profile: they hold large volumes of high-impact corporate data where exposure carries severe regulatory, legal, or market consequences," Unit 42 said. "These attackers targeted financial services organizations about twice as often as healthcare organizations in August 2026, but healthcare overtook financial services in September."

The activity has been attributed to a number of threat clusters, including -

Bling Libra (aka ShinyHunters)

CL-CRI-1116 (aka Redact, formerly BlackFile), first observed in January 2026

CL-CRI-1147 (aka Pink), first observed in May 2026

CL-CRI-1182 (aka Falcon), first observed in mid-2026

CL-CRI-1192 (aka Helix and UNC6671), first observed in June 2026

CL-CRI-1264 (aka Vortex), first observed in September 2026

CL-CRI-1274 (aka Cinder), first observed in September 2026

Intrusions typically commence with voice phishing, with the attackers posing as IT staff to resolve a purported authentication issue and direct the victim to a phishing page personalized to their organization.

Once the AiTM kit captures credentials and session tokens, the attackers quickly enroll their own multi-factor authentication (MFA) devices, abuse cloud utilities and, in a few instances, deploy malicious browser extensions to exfiltrate data and extort victims.

The harvested authentication data is used to authenticate to victim environments, typically by routing such requests through residential proxy networks and commercial VPNs. They then seek to establish multi-layered persistence by registering their MFA devices, generating cloud infrastructure temporary credentials, authorizing unapproved third-party OAuth applications; enumerate sensitive data; evade defenses by modifying mailbox rules and clearing security alert events; and harvest data from cloud platforms.

"Attackers associated with CL-CRI-1264 establish initial access through vishing, where they pose as internal IT support and direct employees to servers using subdomains tailored to each targeted organization," Unit 42 said.

"These servers act as adversary-in-the-middle (AitM) proxies that pass user credentials and multi-factor authentication (MFA) approvals to the attackers in real time. Attackers have targeted employees on both their corporate workstations and personal mobile devices."

CL-CRI-1264 has also been observed enrolling unauthorized MFA devices within minutes of authenticating to establish persistence, often reusing a single device named Pixel 10a across compromised accounts. The attack chains involve automated data exfiltration using Python scripts to harvest information from Microsoft 365 mailbox, OneDrive, and SharePoint.

Once data is exfiltrated, the threat actor sends Microsoft Teams messages directly to employees at victim organizations, claiming data theft and instructing them to visit a TOR-hosted ransom negotiation site.

The second new cluster to emerge around the same time is CL-CRI-1274, which has also leaned on vishing for initial access and leveraged the Microsoft Graph software development kit (SDK) to collect and exfiltrate data from the victim's SharePoint and OneDrive environments.

"Afterwards, the attackers used a compromised account to send extortion messages internally via email and Microsoft Teams, ultimately directing recipients to a Tor-hosted negotiation site," Unit 42 said.

The cybersecurity company said it also observed the CL-CRI-1192 attackers using a malicious browser extension named "Google Chrome" that's said to have been sideloaded onto the browser after enabling Developer Mode. The add-on performs a number of malicious actions, such as -

Installing global keyloggers

Scanning pages for QR codes to capture login/MFA tokens

Streaming webcam feeds

Implementing a command-and-control (C2) remote control module via Chrome DevTools Protocol to capture screenshots, dispatch input events, steal clipboard contents, and allow remote site navigation.

"We assess with moderate confidence that the attackers associated with CL-CRI-1192 developed this malicious browser extension as an alternative method to extract credentials from desktop and laptop devices running Chromium-based browsers such as Google Chrome and Microsoft Edge," Unit 42 said.

"The extension might also be shared with and used by other Com-affiliated attackers, although we currently have no evidence to suggest this."

One of the defining traits of the threat actors is their use of "apex" domains that impersonate single sign-on (SSO) portals, IT support pages, and passkey configuration services for each of the targeted organizations.

Also shared among the attackers is an AitM phishing kit called Doko's Panel that allows them to steal authenticated sessions and then abuse them to access connected software-as-a-service (SaaS) platforms like SharePoint, Salesforce, DocuSign, and Slack to exfiltrate data of interest.

"The same persona behind Doko's Panel has been observed actively recruiting 'experienced callers' via Telegram, specifying requirements including fluent English with no accent, and advertising six-to-seven-figure weekly returns — evidence of the professionalized vishing-as-a-service model that complements the phishing kit ecosystem," Push Security noted in a report published earlier this May.

Microsoft has also revealed cloud-based intrusions in which threat actors have employed passkey and SSO lures to obtain unauthorized access and add their own authentication methods to set up persistence, as well as conduct high-volume Microsoft Graph activity, SharePoint and OneDrive downloads, and mailbox collection through REST APIs.

"Rather than employing custom tools, the threat actors primarily leveraged native cloud utilities and selectively limited executions and persistence deployments," Unit 42 said. "Cybercriminals like Bling Libra and other Com-affiliated threat actors are pivoting away from encryption, enabling them to move at greater speed across their intrusion lifecycle while still achieving their objectives."

[3] The Hacker News - WordPress Blocks Six Backdoored WP Connect Plugins Before Malicious Releases Go Live
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Wed, 25 Nov 2026 11:51:12 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/wordpress-blocks-six-backdoored-wp.html

[TAM METİN - 574 kelime]
WordPress has removed six WordPress plugins developed by WP Connect after they were backdoored on November 22, 2026, to enable arbitrary code execution on sites that have them installed, following a suspected compromise of the developer's account.

The WordPress plugins team has independently detected and blocked the threat before it could be officially released. Visiting each plugin listing now serves the message: "This plugin has been closed as of November 22, 2026, and is not available for download. This closure is permanent."

The names of the affected plugins and the malicious versions are listed below -

Air WP Sync – Airtable to WordPress (2.9.1 – 2.9.4)

WP Sync for Notion – Notion to WordPress (1.7.4)

Add-on Brevo for Gravity Forms (2.6.3)

Add-On for Contact Form 7 to Airtable (2.5.4)

Add-On for Contact Form 7 to Notion (1.6.4)

Orders Sync to Airtable for WooCommerce (1.0.2)

These plugins have been collectively installed on more than 3,600 WordPress sites, according to WordPress security company Wordfence. The supply chain compromise is being tracked as CVE-2026-135202 (CVSS score: 9.8). It remains unpatched.

"Each release silently loaded the same remote-control module, which phones home to an attacker-controlled server, executes any PHP code it is sent, and exposes a full command set for running shell commands, stealing password hashes, and planting webshells," security researcher Chloe Chamberland said.

Specifically, the plugins come with a backdoor module ("includes/airwp-online.php") that runs on every request, sends site information (site URL, PHP, WordPress, and OS versions, and the server user) to a hard-coded command-and-control server ("tascredit-pay[.]com"), and runs PHP code returned by the server via eval().

It can also perform the following actions -

Accept queued commands that run shell commands

Read, write, and delete arbitrary files

Query arbitrary database tables

Dump the users table including login, email address, and password hashes

Drop a web shell into the uploads directory or to a path of the attacker's choosing

Register an hourly WP-Cron hook that runs attacker-supplied code

As a result, an unauthenticated attacker in control of the site can execute arbitrary code on the server and take complete control, facilitating credential theft and persistence through web shells and cron hooks that outlast the plugin removal.

Because the malicious versions were never released and blocked due to WordPress's new cooldown protections as part of its Protect The Shire initiative, sites running these plugins are unlikely to be affected. However, it's essential that site owners check their websites to make sure that they are not running one of the compromised versions and remove them as soon as possible.

"Unlike a typical vulnerability, this was not a coding mistake, and there is no patch: the malicious code was added deliberately and committed to each plugin's WordPress.org repository," Chamberland. "Taken together, this strongly suggests that the developer's WordPress.org account was compromised and used to push the malicious releases, rather than that the vendor shipped this code intentionally."

The disclosure comes as URLscan.io revealed that an unspecified WordPress site had been infected by multiple cybercriminal campaigns, with attackers attempting to disable or remove scripts belonging to competing threat actors.

The site carried "thirteen separate ClickFix injections from three distinct kits [LOM, ErrTraffic, and Macfinger], alongside seven further malicious loaders," it said. "Several of the injections attempt to remove each other's code and overlays, and one injection disables another through a JavaScript naming collision. Five of the thirteen injections resolve their next-stage infrastructure from Polygon smart contracts, a technique known as EtherHiding."

[4] The Hacker News - Iran-Aligned Volant Manticore Uses IPFS-Based KuboShell Backdoor in Espionage Campaigns
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Tue, 24 Nov 2026 22:24:07 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/iran-aligned-volant-manticore-uses-ipfs.html

[TAM METİN - 883 kelime]
A previously undocumented Iran-aligned cyber espionage group called Volant Manticore has targeted telecommunications companies, airlines, and critical infrastructure in Cyprus.

Central to the group's operations is a backdoor called KuboShell that uses the InterPlanetary File System (IPFS) for command-and-control (C2) and deploys Homa, a post-compromise framework that's a reimplementation of MuddyWater's MuddyC2Go. Evidence indicates that the threat actor has been operational since late August 2025.

Also delivered alongside these tools are Remote Monitoring and Management (RMM) programs like MeshCentral and NetLock RMM. In tandem, the group has been observed orchestrating credential harvesting campaigns to collect email and corporate credentials of some of the impersonated organizations.

The use of legitimate RMM tools for persistent access has been a hallmark of the MuddyWater group, although the adversary has since also increasingly deployed its own malware in attacks. Both MuddyWater and Volant Manticore are assessed to be affiliated with Iran's Ministry of Intelligence and Security (MOIS).

"While we identified multiple links to MuddyWater, we assess that Volant Manticore likely consists of former MuddyWater operators who branched off from the group and currently operate as a separate threat cluster," Check Point said in an analysis published today. "Volant Manticore's targeting priorities appear responsive to regional geopolitical developments."

This assessment is based on the use of an older MuddyWater framework and its playbook, while diverging from established modus operandi over time to incorporate its own attack methods like credential-harvesting campaigns. The targeting of Cyprus, a country that does not align with MuddyWater's targeting patterns, and the use of separate infrastructure points to a group that pursues its own strategic objectives.

Attack chains have been found to leverage artificial intelligence (AI)-generated personas in bogus CVs to trick targets into clicking on a link to download the complete resume, resulting in the deployment of KuboShell on the victim's system. The activity is said to have been aimed at a Cyprus-based airline that operates flights between Cyprus and Israel.

This includes a shift toward Saudi Arabian healthcare entities, coinciding with escalating hostilities between Saudi Arabia and the Iranian-backed Houthis.

"This fits the MOIS's track record of targeting hospitals and healthcare providers through cyber operations during military conflicts, in some cases attempting to disrupt their operations," the cybersecurity company said.

As recently as September 2026, the group has been found to launch a phishing campaign against Saudi targets, distributing the backdoor and N-able RMM through two different healthcare-themed ZIP archives named "AI_Healthcare_Seminar.zip" and "Ai_Healthcare_Seminar_Online.zip," respectively. Some of the emails have been sent from a compromised account belonging to a private hospital in Saudi Arabia.

A Windows backdoor, KuboShell uses IPFS for C2 and relies on Pinata to facilitate file transfer over the peer-to-peer (P2P) file-sharing network.

"On startup, KuboShell checks whether it has administrator privileges. If not, it tries to relaunch itself with higher privileges, which triggers a User Account Control (UAC) prompt," Check Point explained.

"KuboShell retrieves the MAC address of the machine's first network adapter and uses it as a unique identifier for each victim. It then registers the host by uploading a file containing hi, saved to C:\Users\Public\<MAC>.log, using a hard-coded Pinata access token."

Once registered, KuboShell checks Pinata for a command file named <MAC>.txt, and if the file exists, looks up the file’s IPFS identifier and downloads its contents through Pinata's public gateway. The downloaded content is executed via PowerShell in a hidden window. The results of the execution are saved in a file named "C:\Users\Public\<MAC>re.txt" and uploaded back to the server.

It's worth noting that the backdoor is not responsible for capturing the output. Rather, the results are returned only if the received command writes them to the text file. KuboShell then checks for a new command every 60 seconds.

Analysis indicates that KuboShell is primarily used to run basic reconnaissance commands and assess the compromised environment before downloading and executing a more capable backdoor, which includes the modular Homa Framework. This involves a three-stage process -

PowerShell stager, which contacts a hard-coded IP address ("185.205.187[.]109:8000") to fetch a PowerShell loader and execute it in memory

PowerShell loader, which decodes and launches a JavaScript backdoor

JavaScript Backdoor, which implements a continuous C2 loop to receive and execute PowerShell, SSH, and "cmd.exe" commands

It's suspected that the third-stage backdoor is used to execute additional modules, although the precise nature of those components is presently unknown.

"We assess that Homa's client is a JavaScript reimplementation of the older MuddyC2Go client, potentially allowing Volant Manticore to retain the more complex server-side component with minimal modifications, thereby reducing development time and resources," Check Point said.

The post-compromise framework has also been distributed via a macro-enabled Microsoft Word document called "MilitaryHeritageProposal.doc" that's believed to have targeted a Cypriot defense-related government entity. Enabling the content triggers the execution of a VBA backdoor that serves as a conduit for running follow-up payloads or commands using "cmd.exe."

"Volant Manticore shows how Iran's cyber ecosystem continues to reshape itself," Check Point concluded. "What appears to be a group of former MuddyWater operators now operates independently, pairing legacy MuddyWater tooling with new implants of their own while pursuing a regional focus that has until now drawn little attention."

"If MuddyC2Go is the latest MuddyWater framework Volant Manticore's operators had access to, the split may have happened around 2023 as MuddyWater replaced MuddyC2Go with DarkBeatC2 in late 2023.

[5] The Hacker News - VMware vCenter Hit by Another Critical 9.8-Rated Auth Bypass Flaw After Zero-Day Exploitation
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Tue, 24 Nov 2026 18:58:26 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/vmware-vcenter-hit-by-another-critical.html

[TAM METİN - 335 kelime]
Broadcom has shipped patches to address 23 vulnerabilities impacting VMware ESX, vCenter, Workstation, Fusion, and Avi Load Balancer, including one critical bug that could be abused to bypass authentication.

The critical vulnerability, tracked as CVE-2026-123903, is rated 9.8 out of 10.0 on the CVSS scoring system. It resides in the VMware Directory Service ("vmdir").

"A malicious actor with network access to vCenter may exploit this issue to bypass authentication and gain unauthorized access to the system," Broadcom said in an advisory released today.

The company credited sglab of QiAnXin, Qi Qin (@LeoQ7_) of depthfirst, and VulTeam and XGTP from ThreatBook for independently reporting the flaw.

Also patched in vCenter are four high-severity flaws, including an arbitrary file write vulnerability while processing certain archives that could be exploited to achieve code execution on the appliance (CVE-2026-123904, CVSS score: 7.5).

The remaining three vulnerabilities relate to denial-of-service in the VMware Directory Service (CVE-2026-123905, CVE-2026-123906, and CVE-2026-123907, CVSS scores: 7.5).

The issues have been addressed in the following versions -

VMware Cloud Foundation and VMware vSphere Foundation 9.1.x.x - Fixed in 9.1.2

VMware Cloud Foundation and VMware vSphere Foundation 9.0.x.x - Fixed in 9.0.2.0300

VMware vCenter 8.0 - Fixed in 8.0 U3m

VMware vCenter 7.0 (Contact Broadcom Support, if on extended support contract)

VMware Cloud Foundation 5.x - Fixed in 5.2.5

VMware Telco Cloud Platform - Fixed in vCenter 8.0 U3m (TCI: 3.0, TCP: 3.0, 4.x, 5.0.x, 5.1.x, 5.2)

VMware Telco Cloud Infrastructure - Fixed in vCenter 8.0 U3m (TCI: 3.0, TCP: 3.0, 4.x, 5.0.x, 5.1.x, 5.2)

The fixes arrive days after Broadcom shipped updates for CVE-2026-123890 (CVSS score: 9.8), another authentication bypass flaw impacting the VMware Directory Service that was exploited as a zero-day by a suspected China-nexus cyber espionage group called Armory Castle. VMware has noted that CVE-2026-123903 is a distinct issue and is not related to CVE-2026-123890.

While Broadcom makes no mention of any of the newly disclosed flaws as being exploited in the wild, it's essential that customers apply the updates for optimal protection.

[6] The Hacker News - Okta Links ShinyHunters-Associated Vishing Group to New INCEPTION Data Leak Site
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Tue, 24 Nov 2026 18:58:21 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/okta-links-shinyhunters-associated.html

[TAM METİN - 903 kelime]
A threat cluster previously assessed to be affiliated with the ShinyHunters extortion crew may have rebranded and continued operations under a new name called INCEPTION, according to Okta's Threat Intelligence team.

The cluster, dubbed O-UNC-053, is characterized by the use of voice phishing (aka vishing) attacks to trick targets into entering credentials in phishing pages and approving multi-factor authentication (MFA). Okta said it's been tracking the group since March 2026.

The identity and access management provider said targets of this cluster previously had stolen data published to the ShinyHunters data leak site (DLS). However, as of November 16, 2026, a new data leak site operating under the name INCEPTION has listed the data siphoned from O-UNC-053 targets.

"While this threat cluster has changed their brand affiliation, the operators remain active in targeting organizations with voice phishing attacks and account takeovers resulting in data theft and extortion," Okta researchers Moussa Diallo and Katie Nickels said in a report shared with The Hacker News.

Social engineering and credential harvesting attacks mounted by O-UNC-053 have targeted a broad range of sectors, including technology, healthcare, biotechnology, financial services, insurance, entertainment, gaming, retail, transportation, hospitality, and legal.

Similar to O-UNC-066 and UNC6671, the newly identified cluster also relies on a phishing kit to intercept authentication flows and capture a user's credentials and MFA codes. However, the kit is not designed to bypass phishing-resistant methods like passkeys and Okta FastPass.

"While interacting with targeted users on the phone, the operators socially engineer them into sharing factors like one-time passcodes and approving push notifications," Okta said. "If successful, the operators gain access to SaaS applications and exfiltrate sensitive data, which is often used to extort targets under the threat of releasing it on a data leak site."

Domains created by the group are registered through NiceNIC, Spaceship, Tucows, and NameSilo, with the domain names using the following patterns -

legaldept-<target>[.]com

The use of "legal" themes in these attacks is said to have started in October 2026, and is often registered on or within a day of a campaign starting. Prior campaign waves have employed help desk-, support-, and claims-related themes for the registered domains.

These findings also dovetail with an alert from Palo Alto Networks Unit 42, which has warned of widespread vishing activity using legal-themed domains that either begin with "legal-" or end with "-legal" using the ".com" top-level domain.

More than 70 such domains have been observed since October 2026, it said, adding each domain is specifically created for an intended target operating in different industries, like cryptocurrency, financial services, healthcare, high technology, and hospitality, among others.

"Registered in rapid evening batches and shielded behind Cloudflare, these web-only domains deploy a filtered kit that delivers tailored content to intended victims while routing visitors it is not configured to attack to the targeted organization's legitimate Okta or NetIQ sign-in flow," ReversingLabs researcher Robert Simmons said.

Some of the confirmed domains linked to the activity are listed below -

activisionblizzard-legal[.]com

legal-westernunion[.]com

legal-creditkarma[.]com

The crux of the campaign is to tailor the landing page to the specific identity provider of the target, while filtering unwanted visitors and forwarding them to the organization's authentication infrastructure, such as Okta.

"Throughout our tracking of O-UNC-053 between March and September 2026, we observed the names of targeted entities appear on the ShinyHunters DLS between 1 and 28 days after the phishing infrastructure was registered," Okta noted.

However, it's unclear at this stage if the published data on the INCEPTION site is authentic and whether they are running the site themselves or are collaborating with another extortion gang. The exact nature of the relationship between ShinyHunters and O-UNC-053 is also unknown.

It's worth noting that the ShinyHunters data leak site went offline in late September 2026 following the arrest of three alleged members of the group, including a Canadian co-conspirator responsible for the breach of the FBIjobs.gov portal.

There is no evidence of data from O-UNC-053 targets being published on any data leak site from early September 2026 through October and early November 2026, until listings for multiple targets appeared on the INCEPTION site last week.

"This gap in publishing on any leak site followed by publishing on a new data leak site with a new brand is consistent with prior shifts from Com-affiliated actors," Okta said. "Regardless of the nature of the relationship with the INCEPTION DLS, O-UNC-053 remains active in conducting vishing attacks against targets in an effort to take over accounts, steal data, and extort targets."

INCEPTION Denies ShinyHunters Link

In a post on their dark web site, INCEPTION has denied any affiliation with ShinyHunters, insisting that it operates independently with its own members and objectives.

"We are not ShinyHunters. We were never ShinyHunters. We are INCEPTION," the group said, adding "certain parties in the threat intelligence industry have been eager to give us a history we never asked for."

"We are also not copying anyone. If our methodology resembles what came before, it is because we took inspiration from operators who, whatever else may be said of them, understood that the weakest component of any secure system is a human being holding a phone. That is not theft of tradecraft. It is acknowledgment of a good idea -- the same way every group after LockBit used a leak site, and nobody called them LockBit."

(The story was updated after publication to include a statement posted by INCEPTION on its dark web site.)

[7] The Hacker News - Hackers Found a Way Around GitHub SHA Pinning. This Webinar Explains How to Close the Gap
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Tue, 24 Nov 2026 18:12:09 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/hackers-found-way-around-github-sha.html

[TAM METİN - 618 kelime]
Pinning GitHub Actions to an exact version of their code, known as SHA pinning, has become the go-to defense against software supply chain attacks. Attackers have already found a way around it.

In March 2026, the hackers who hijacked Trivy, a widely used open-source security scanner, planted malicious code that looked like it came from GitHub's official checkout tool. They even labeled it a security fix. Teams pinned to it would have passed every check.

The trick works because GitHub lets copies of a project, called forks, share code with the original. In May, the same technique hit the popular actions-cool/issues-helper tool, stealing credentials from the pipelines that ran it.

The lesson: pinning guarantees the code won't change, but not who wrote it.

That gap is the focus of an upcoming webinar, GitHub Actions Security Beyond SHA Pinning. If your team treats pinning as the finish line, save your seat to learn what it actually protects you from, and what it doesn't.

Immutability Is Not Provenance

Pinning earned its reputation. When the tj-actions/changed-files compromise repointed tags to a malicious commit across more than 23,000 repositories in March 2025, teams that had pinned to SHAs were unaffected.

GitHub now lets administrators enforce pinning as an organization-wide policy, and few security teams argue against it.

📅 Save Your Spot: Learn Why “Best Practice” Won’t Stop the Next GitHub Actions Attack

But a pinned SHA leaves three questions unanswered:

Authorship: A SHA doesn't tell you who wrote the commit. Any fork commit can be pinned.

Project membership: Org-level enforcement checks that a reference is a 40-character SHA, not that the commit actually lives in the upstream project.

Runtime: A Docker-based action pinned to a SHA can still build its image at runtime, pulling in dependencies that were never frozen.

With agentic development pushing more code through CI than ever, each of those gaps gets exercised more often and reviewed less closely.

GitHub's Lockfiles Help. They Don't Finish the Job.

GitHub's 2026 Actions security roadmap, published March 26, put deterministic dependency locking front and center. Workflow lockfiles entered public preview on October 14 and became generally available on October 28, 2026. They tie every run to recorded commits for actions and their transitive dependencies, even if a tag moves later.

That's real progress. Locked commits must exist on a branch of the source repository, which blunts fork-only impostors. But GitHub's own changelog is clear that locking "does not determine whether the dependency itself is safe." Commit authorship and runtime builds remain open; immutable releases only help when maintainers opt in, and lockfiles aren't currently supported on GitHub Enterprise Server.

Not sure where your pipelines fall in that gap? Register for the webinar for a clear-eyed breakdown of what GitHub's roadmap fixes and what it still leaves to you.

Led by Erika Heidi, Staff Developer Relations Engineer at Chainguard, the session draws a hard line between immutability and provenance, and covers:

What org-level pin enforcement actually verifies, and the false confidence it can create

How impostor commits work, and why GitHub's repository network makes them possible

What GitHub's Actions roadmap addresses, and what it doesn't

Other ways to close the trust gap, including a curated catalog of vetted actions

Platform and DevOps engineers who own CI/CD, AppSec and DevSecOps teams writing pipeline policy, and security leaders who signed off on SHA pinning as "done." If your workflows pull in third-party actions, this session is for you.

Attackers have already shown they can disguise a malicious commit as a hardening fix. The next one won't announce itself either.

Register now for GitHub Actions Security Beyond SHA Pinning, pick the session time that works for you, and find out what your pins aren't telling you.

[8] The Hacker News - Google Researcher Shows How a Single Bit Change in .NET Files Bypassed Windows Code Integrity
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Tue, 24 Nov 2026 17:32:00 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/google-researcher-shows-how-single-bit.html

[TAM METİN - 773 kelime]
A quirk in how the .NET Framework loads a program file let a local attacker run untrusted code past two separate protections on Windows built to block it, Google Project Zero researcher James Forshaw showed in a write-up published yesterday.

The same loading behavior bypassed Oracle VirtualBox's process hardening on Windows hosts, tracked as CVE-2026-87268, and Windows code integrity, tracked as CVE-2026-108398.

Oracle fixed the VirtualBox flaw in September, and Microsoft fixed the Windows one on October 13, both before the write-up appeared.

VirtualBox on Windows monitors the system calls that load program files into its virtual machine processes, so a less-trusted user on the same computer cannot push code into them. Forshaw got past that watch through the .NET Framework.

The framework can run a .NET program file from a plain copy in memory when the normal way of loading it, as an executable image, fails. Version 4 of the framework takes that fallback on one specific error, and the memory copy skips the image checks a loaded program would face.

The VirtualBox check returned that error for any .NET file compiled as AnyCPU, a form meant to run on any processor, so an unchanged file loaded through the path the hardening never inspects.

To get the file loaded while a virtual machine was started, he registered a COM object in the current user's settings that a protected VirtualBox process would pull in, a method known as COM hijacking. He set out the mechanism in his technical analysis.

Oracle rates the flaw, CVE-2026-87268, as a local problem that needs low privileges and no user interaction, scores it 7.8, and lists VirtualBox 7.2.16 on Windows hosts as affected. Its security advisory says a successful attack can result in the "takeover of Oracle VM VirtualBox."

The code runs inside the protected VirtualBox process. It is not a break out of a guest virtual machine onto the host, and the attacker begins as an ordinary user on that host.

The same idea worked against Windows' own code-integrity checks, which decide what user-mode code is allowed to run. There is no VirtualBox hook here, only the system call that maps a file as an image.

The researcher found that clearing a single bit in a .NET file's header, the IMAGE_FILE_EXECUTABLE_IMAGE flag, made that call reject the file while .NET still loaded and ran it.

"This single bit change is all you need to load an untrusted .NET assembly," he wrote. He tried it on Windows 11 S Mode, which enables User Mode Code Integrity (UMCI), the policy that limits which user-mode programs can run. A logic error in .NET's own policy check let the file load even with UMCI enforced.

The flaw, CVE-2026-108398, works through the .NET Framework version 4 loading path and needs a program willing to load the assembly. It is not a way to seize any Windows machine over the internet.

Microsoft rates it a local, low-privilege security-feature bypass, scores it 8.8, and fixed it on October 13. The company lists it as publicly disclosed but not used in attacks.

The same file reached a more sensitive target as well. Protected Process Light, or PPL, is a kind of Windows process that ordinary programs, even ones with administrator rights, are not meant to enter. By hijacking a COM object that WerSecureFault.exe loads, he ran his test code inside a PPL process at the highest protection level without administrator rights.

The older Protected Process type resisted the technique because the .NET runtime files lack the page hashes it requires. The October fix corrects the policy check for UMCI but does not change loading for protected processes, so that PPL route should still work, according to the researcher. It carries no separate CVE.

On Windows hosts, update VirtualBox to a build that carries the September fixes. The affected versions are 7.2.16 and 7.2.18, and 7.2.18 shipped on September 15 with that set. An upstream code fix is not the same as a released binary, so check the running version rather than assume an older build already has it.

.NET systems need the October 13 update that matches the exact Windows and .NET Framework version in use, not one shared package. The update release notes list the right KB for each. On Windows 11 version 26H2, for example, it is KB5127694.

Forshaw first presented the Windows code-integrity bypass at the 44Con conference in September, having judged it fell outside Microsoft's rules for what gets fixed. After .NET developers told him the behavior he had counted on was accidental, he reported it in late September. The fix arrived in October's Patch Tuesday, less than a month later.

[9] The Hacker News - 2027 SOC Budget Reset: 5 Priorities to Cut Costs and Reduce Cyber Risk
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Tue, 24 Nov 2026 17:31:54 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/2027-soc-budget-reset-5-priorities-to.html

[TAM METİN - 1088 kelime]
What if your biggest SOC budget problem isn't how much you spend, but how much analyst time you lose?

According to ANY.RUN's estimates, SOC teams could recover 450 analyst hours per 1,000 investigated alerts through faster threat analysis and IOC research. That's a significant opportunity as manual investigations, repeated checks, and unnecessary escalations continue to drain resources.

As 2027 approaches, here are five priorities to reduce SOC costs, recover capacity, and strengthen protection against costly cyberattacks.

Why 2027 SOC Budget Planning Is Getting More Challenging

Cybersecurity spending is facing conflicting pressures. According to IANS and Artico Search, 55% of CISOs reported flat or declining budgets in 2026. Meanwhile, PwC's 2027 Global Digital Trust Insights found that 84% of surveyed security and finance leaders expect cybersecurity budgets to increase.

For SOC leaders, this creates several challenges:

Growing workloads: More alerts and complex threats demand additional investigation capacity.

Rising operational costs: Manual processes, staffing needs, and expanding security infrastructure put pressure on budgets.

Pressure to prove ROI: Every investment must demonstrate measurable improvements in security and operational efficiency.

Increasing cyber risks: Cost reductions cannot come at the expense of detection coverage or response capabilities.

1. Prioritize AI That Delivers Measurable SOC Value

AI is becoming a major cybersecurity investment priority, but adding AI capabilities won't automatically improve SOC efficiency. For 2027, the focus should be on reducing investigation costs, improving analyst productivity, and avoiding unnecessary escalations.

For example, ANY.RUN's Interactive Sandbox combines automated threat analysis with AI-powered reporting to help teams achieve these goals:

Cut manual investigation time: Automated Interactivity follows redirects, extracts QR code links, and handles supported CAPTCHAs, exposing malicious activity that would otherwise require additional analyst effort.

Reduce investigation and escalation costs: AI-powered Tier 1 reports provide clear summaries of malicious behavior and recommended next steps. This helps analysts make faster decisions and reduces the need for repeated investigations and unnecessary Tier 2 involvement.

These improvements have measurable business value. ANY.RUN reports up to 20% lower Tier 1 workload and 30% fewer Tier 1-to-Tier 2 escalations, helping SOCs handle growing workloads with less pressure to expand their teams.

The 2027 budget priority: Evaluate AI investments based on investigation time, workload reduction, and escalation rates, not the number of AI features included.

Make Your 2027 AI Budget Deliver Measurable Results

Reduce Tier 1 workload by up to 20% and control investigation costs.

Cut SOC Investigation Costs

2. Recover Analyst Capacity Before Expanding Your Team

As alert volumes grow, hiring additional analysts may seem like the obvious solution. But before expanding headcount, SOCs should calculate how much capacity they could recover by improving investigation efficiency.

ANY.RUN estimates that faster behavioral analysis through its Interactive Sandbox, combined with less manual threat research, can save 450 analyst hours per 1,000 investigated alerts.

Consider a SOC handling 4,000 alerts annually:

Illustrative calculation based on ANY.RUN's estimate and an assumed hourly rate. Actual results depend on SOC workloads and investigation processes.

The $108,000 represents recovered analyst capacity, not guaranteed cash savings. However, these hours could help reduce overtime, limit outsourcing, or defer additional hiring.

The budget priority: Compare projected alert volumes, investigation workloads, and staffing requirements before approving additional headcount. Recovering existing capacity can help SOCs handle more threats without proportional increases in personnel costs.

3. Reduce IOC Research Costs with Connected Threat Context

Investigating a suspicious IOC often means searching multiple sources to uncover related infrastructure, previous attacks, and malicious activity. These repeated checks consume analyst time and increase investigation costs.

Used by 700,000+ security professionals across 16,000+ organizations, ANY.RUN helps SOC teams benefit from threat intelligence generated through shared malware and phishing investigations.

Its Interactive Sandbox provides Related Threat Context, connecting discovered IOCs to previously observed threats. Analysts can pivot directly into Threat Intelligence Lookup to explore related IPs, domains, files, and attack infrastructure.

This connected approach helps teams identify broader attack patterns faster, avoid repeated research, and make better-informed investigation decisions.

The budget priority: Reduce time spent on manual IOC enrichment and threat correlation. Faster access to relevant intelligence means lower investigation overhead and more capacity to investigate critical threats.

4. Get More Detection Value from Your Existing Security Stack

Before investing in additional security products, organizations should consider how effectively their existing systems use threat intelligence.

Even advanced SIEM and SOAR platforms can miss emerging threats if they lack fresh indicators. Integrating real-time intelligence into existing workflows can help teams:

Detect threats earlier: Identify malicious activity using recently discovered IOCs.

Reduce operational costs: Automate indicator collection and minimize manual detection-rule maintenance.

Maximize existing investments: Improve detection coverage without purchasing additional security products.

For example, ANY.RUN's Threat Intelligence Feeds deliver malicious IPs, domains, and URLs through STIX/TAXII, APIs, and supported SIEM/SOAR integrations.

According to ANY.RUN's internal research, up to 30% of unique malicious IOCs found in studied Sandbox analyses were already available in its TI Feeds, providing an opportunity to detect related threats earlier.

The budget priority: Strengthen intelligence coverage within your existing infrastructure before allocating funds to additional security products.

5. Prioritize Investments That Reduce the Financial Impact of Cyberattacks

Cutting SOC expenses means little if slower detection increases the risk of costly incidents. For 2027, investments should be evaluated against their ability to prevent attacks from escalating into serious breaches.

According to IBM's 2026 Cost of a Data Breach Report, the average financial-sector data breach costs $6.3 million.

Based on industry breach-cost research, ANY.RUN's internal risk model estimates potential avoided losses starting at $60,000 per qualifying phishing or malware incident in financial organizations through earlier detection and containment.

While this is a modeled estimate rather than guaranteed savings, it highlights why preventing incident escalation matters when allocating security budgets.

The budget priority: Assess investments based on their ability to improve detection, shorten response times, and reduce potential financial exposure.

Make Every SOC Investment Count in 2027

A stronger SOC doesn't necessarily require a larger budget. The real opportunity lies in making smarter investment decisions that improve security while keeping operational costs under control.

These five priorities can help organizations:

Reduce operational costs by eliminating repetitive investigations and unnecessary manual work.

Increase SOC capacity without proportional increases in staffing.

Get more value from existing security investments through connected threat intelligence and stronger detection.

Reduce financial exposure by improving threat detection and response before attacks escalate.

For 2027, the goal is clear: invest where improvements can be measured, eliminate unnecessary expenses, and build a SOC that delivers stronger protection with greater efficiency.

Get More Value from Your 2027 SOC Budget

Cut SOC costs and cyber risks with your existing resources

[10] The Hacker News - Microsoft Fixes Azure API Management Classic Flaw as Actor Targets Customer Secrets
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Tue, 24 Nov 2026 16:14:49 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/microsoft-fixes-azure-api-management.html

[TAM METİN - 374 kelime]
Norway's National Security Authority (NSM) has disclosed that it became aware of a security vulnerability in Microsoft's Azure API Management (APIM) that has affected multiple businesses in the country, including energy companies Equinor and Skagerak Energi.

"An unknown actor has been actively looking for access tokens, secrets, and named values ​​in APIM," NSM said. "It is still uncertain how extensive the exploitation of the vulnerability is, and has been."

The security agency said it has so far seen no evidence that has had any impact on Norwegian businesses.

According to a non-public advisory issued by Microsoft, the issue is said to affect the APIM classic tier. Instances on v2 tiers are not affected.

"On 18 November 2026, Microsoft identified a security issue affecting Azure API Management Classic tiers that could allow unauthorized access to certain management functionality if the service was not configured according to documented security best practices," Microsoft said in the advisory.

"Microsoft closed the access path on 19 November 2026. Our investigation found that customers were exposed and an unauthorized party could have used the issue to access secrets stored in Azure API Management Classic instances before Microsoft identified the security issue."

As a result, credentials or configuration secrets associated with the API Management resources may have been accessed by an unauthorized third-party if the service has not been configured as per security best practices. The information that may have been accessed could include -

Secrets stored in Named Values Client secrets associated with identity providers

Client secrets associated with OpenID Connect providers

Tokens used to call direct management APIs

As of November 21, 2026, the Windows maker has released guidance for customers to help identify and rotate the secrets and credentials configured in their Azure API Management instances. It did not disclose how many customers were affected.

"If someone obtains a credential from that configuration, they may be able to use it directly against the service it protects," security researcher Anders Kristiansen said. "What they can reach depends on the credential's permissions and whether it is still valid."

NSM has also warned that the vulnerability affects several sectors in Norway and other entities outside of the country, adding it's working with national and international partners and businesses about the matter.

[11] The Hacker News - WHMCS SQL Injection Could Expose Customer Data and Admin Password Hashes without Login
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Tue, 24 Nov 2026 14:04:20 +0530
Link: https://thehackernews.com/2026/11/whmcs-sql-injection-flaw-could-expose.html

[TAM METİN - 506 kelime]
A flaw in WHMCS's shopping cart, the billing and automation platform many web hosting companies use to run their businesses, could allow anyone on the internet to retrieve customer records and administrator password hashes from an installation's database. No login is needed.

The company disclosed the SQL injection yesterday along with two other flaws that need a logged-in client, and released fixed versions. It has not reported attacks using any of the three.

Because no login is needed, the flaw, tracked as CVE-2026-82981 (CVSS score: 8.7), is within reach of anyone who can load the shopping cart of a vulnerable installation over the internet.

The records an attacker could pull include clients' personal and support information as well as admin accounts with their hashed passwords, according to the company's advisory. A stolen hash is not a working password, but it gives attackers something to try to crack offline.

The 9.0.10 and 8.13.8 updates shipped yesterday, and the release notes list them as maintenance releases. For the 9.1 series, the security advisory names 9.1.2 as the fix, but that build had not yet appeared on the company's 9.1 release notes page at publication time.

Fixes are available only for supported versions, so installations on the 8.0 to 8.12 series must upgrade to a fixed release.

Installing the updates falls to self-hosted administrators, since WHMCS Cloud installations are updated automatically, according to the company's updating documentation.

The second SQL injection, CVE-2026-82979 (CVSS score: 7.1), sits in the Client Area and needs a logged-in client that holds the View & Manage Contacts permission. That client could read other customers' personal, billing, and support information, as well as the same admin records and password hashes, the second advisory says.

The third flaw, CVE-2026-93700, lies in how email campaigns process client-supplied data. A logged-in client could use it to read sensitive configuration files on the server, including database credentials, and to run code on the server, its advisory says.

It carries the highest score of the three, 9.0, though its CVE record uses the older CVSS 3.1 scale while the other two use CVSS 4.0. That record's vector indicates that some user interaction is required, and the company has not said what else exploitation entails.

All three flaws were reported by a single researcher who uses the handle alakazamspooon.

For the email-campaign flaw, updating is not the whole job. The email-campaign advisory also tells administrators to:

Delete every planned or ongoing campaign under Utilities > Email Campaigns, using Delete in each campaign's Manage Campaign menu.

Clear the template cache under Utilities > System > System Cleanup, using Empty Template Cache.

Update to a fixed release.

The advisory does not explain why existing campaigns must go, leaving open the question of whether updating alone is enough.

This is at least the third round of WHMCS security fixes this year. The company patched an authorization flaw in the Client Area on May 12, described in its May advisory, and an unauthenticated remote code execution flaw on September 3, described in its September advisory.

​Unknown attackers have reportedly breached an open-source intelligence (OSINT) analysis system at the US Central Intelligence Agency (CIA), according to a source familiar with the matter.

The source told The New York Times, which first reported the incident, that the cyberattack was carried out using the ARTEX AI-powered penetration testing framework and also affected the Office of the Director of National Intelligence (ODNI) and the National Geospatial-Intelligence Agency.

While the CIA and the ODNI have not yet replied to a request for more details regarding this incident, no classified data or systems were exposed in the breach.

"The hack did not involve our classified networks or classified information, and no other systems are currently at risk," the source said.

Hours after the CIA security breach was reported, the Financial Crimes Enforcement Network (FinCEN) also disclosed that it's investigating a cybersecurity incident involving unauthorized access to a limited subset of its systems. After detecting the attack, the agency took some systems offline as a precaution and told banks to use the Financial Institutions Hotline to file reports, since some services may be unavailable.

FinCEN is a US Department of the Treasury bureau that acts as the country's official Financial Intelligence Unit (FIU) and protects the nation's financial system from illicit activities by sharing financial intelligence with law enforcement agencies both domestically and internationally.

BleepingComputer reached out to a FinCEN spokesperson with questions about the incident, but a response was not immediately available.

One week ago, the US National Security Agency's classified network also suffered a days-long outage, but the agency blamed scheduled routine maintenance and said its mission-critical capabilities remained fully operational.

US agencies targeted by cyberattacks

These incidents follow a series of other cyberattacks that have led to security breaches at other US federal agencies in recent years.

For instance, two years ago, the Silk Typhoon Chinese state-backed hacking group hacked the Office of Foreign Assets Control (OFAC), another Treasury Department office that administers economic and trade sanctions programs.

The same threat group also breached the Treasury's Office of Financial Research, and it reportedly infiltrated the Committee on Foreign Investment in the United States (a Treasury Department office that reviews foreign investments for national security risks).

More recently, as BleepingComputer reported over the weekend, hackers reportedly breached the FBI's National Crime Information Center (NCIC). However, it is unclear how the attackers gained access, when the breach occurred, or whether they stole any information from the compromised systems.

This follows a claim by the ShinyHunters extortion group that it breached the FBI's recruitment website, FBIJobs.gov, prompting an FBI investigation that led to the arrests of multiple suspected ShinyHunters members, including a Canadian cybersecurity executive reportedly linked to the attack.

[13] BleepingComputer - Rackspace shuts down Spot cloud platform after security breach
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Tue, 24 Nov 2026 16:37:30 -0500
Link: https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/rackspace-shuts-down-spot-cloud-platform-after-security-breach/

[TAM METİN - 586 kelime]
Rackspace is shutting down its Rackspace Spot cloud platform after a security breach allowed hackers to access customer Kubernetes clusters and secrets, giving customers until December 8 to migrate their services and download their data.

Rackspace Spot is a cloud platform that allows organizations to deploy and manage containerized applications using Kubernetes.

The company announced the shutdown in a new security notification sent to customers and obtained by BleepingComputer, saying it had decided to discontinue the service after reviewing the incident.

"Following a review of the situation, Rackspace has made the decision to wind down the Rackspace Spot service. This decision is being made to provide a clear path forward for customers," reads the notification.

Rackspace says the service will be permanently discontinued on December 8, 2026, at 5 PM Central Time, when all remaining workloads will be terminated, and customer data will no longer be accessible.

Customers have been instructed to retrieve their data and migrate their workloads to different platforms before the deadline.

The decision comes after hackers breached Rackspace Spot on November 17 using compromised credentials, allowing them to create unauthorized pods in customer Kubernetes clusters, read Kubernetes Secrets, and list nodes and volumes.

Rackspace discovered the intrusion on November 18 following a customer report and disabled access to the platform's administration console while securing its infrastructure. Service was restored on November 21 following an extended outage.

"An unauthorized party gained access to parts of the Spot platform and may have read cluster Secrets and files on storage volumes," Rackspace said.

The company says the affected Spot environment is segmented from its core infrastructure and that it has not identified evidence of unauthorized access to its core systems or the environments of other Rackspace customers.

Rackspace is now instructing all Spot customers to treat their API tokens, refresh tokens, kubeconfigs, and Kubernetes Secrets as potentially compromised, regardless of whether they previously received a breach notification.

Customers have also been advised to review access logs dating back to November 16 and search for suspicious Kubernetes objects associated with the attack, including:

IP address: 23.224.79.106

IP address: 23.224.79.106

IP range: 23.224.0.0/15

IP range: 23.224.0.0/15

Suspicious Kubernetes pod and object prefixes: i1, glm-, and probe

Suspicious Kubernetes pod and object prefixes: i1, glm-, and probe

Customers urge Rackspace to reconsider

The shutdown announcement has prompted disappointment among Rackspace Spot customers, with some urging the company to reconsider and offering to pay more to keep the platform operational.

In a Reddit discussion, one customer said they believed users would be willing to pay higher prices to keep the service running.

Other customers expressed similar concerns in a separate discussion, with some asking about affordable alternatives and others describing the impact the shutdown would have on their existing infrastructure.

Others praised the service for its affordability, saying alternative cloud providers would be more expensive.

Rackspace has not indicated whether it will reconsider the shutdown decision or offer customers additional time to migrate their workloads.

[14] BleepingComputer - New Inception extortion gang launches large-scale vishing campaign
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Tue, 24 Nov 2026 15:21:20 -0500
Link: https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/new-inception-extortion-gang-launches-large-scale-vishing-campaign/

[TAM METİN - 576 kelime]
A newly formed data extortion group named ‘Inception’ has been linked to a large-scale campaign that targets single sign-on (SSO) credentials of multiple global brands and firms.

The gang surfaced on November 17, claiming seven victims on its extortion site, including chip maker AMD, PepsiCo, Cigna Healthcare, AutoZone, and Lucid Motors, but none of them have publicly confirmed a data breach.

However, cybersecurity company ReversingLabs has been tracking Inception gang's activity and says that it has been operating since late October.

The security firm has confirmed the existence of 21 domains used for SSO phishing, 41 domains believed to be used in campaign infrastructure, and 4 other domains with suspected use.

The researchers identified an extensive phishing infrastructure impersonating dozens of major corporations, including Dropbox, Duolingo, Western Union, Indeed, Activision Blizzard, Riot Games, Twitch, Reddit, Nestlé, Anthropic, Nintendo, Starbucks, McDonald's, Paramount, and NBCUniversal.

The existence of these domains is not proof that those firms have been breached, or even targeted yet, and none have recently disclosed cyberattacks or other related incidents.

The campaign appears to rely on phone-based social engineering rather than email phishing, strongly resembling the tactics used by the infamous ShinyHunters extortion group.

Inception is using legal-themed websites that imitate corporate authentication services, primarily Okta but also NetIQ/OpenText Access Manager.

ShinyHunters link and denial

One of those authentication providers, Okta, published a report yesterday linking Inception to O-UNC-053, a threat cluster it previously associated with ShinyHunters, which has been active since March.

According to Okta, after law enforcement disrupted the ShinyHunters extortion group following the arrest of suspected members [1, 2], and the shutdown of the leak site, O-UNC-053 continued to register new phishing domains.

In late October, the activity cluster launched its legal-themed campaign, and in mid-November, the Inception leak site went live, announcing victims that matched previous targeting.

“This gap in publishing on any leak site followed by publishing on a new data leak site with a new brand is consistent with prior shifts from Com-affiliated actors,” describes Okta.

“Before and after the change in DLS publication, O-UNC-053 maintained consistent infrastructure and TTPs, leading us to assess that the cluster has very likely changed their brand affiliation.”

Inception was quick to respond and dispute the alleged links to other threat actors in an announcement on their extortion page.

The threat actors claimed to operate independently and with no affiliation to any previous group, arguing that overlapping targets and the use of common domain registration and CDN services did not establish operational links.

They also acknowledged that private tooling is circulating among independent threat actors, and using it does not prove affiliation.

To defend against the latest Inception campaign, ReversingLabs recommends enforcing phishing-resistant authentication, including FIDO2 security keys and passkeys, and strengthening help desk identity verification procedures.

DNS, proxy, and identity provider logs dating back to October 21, 2026, should be carefully reviewed for signs of suspicious activity.

[15] BleepingComputer - Turnitin discloses cyberattack affecting customer-facing systems
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Tue, 24 Nov 2026 13:59:52 -0500
Link: https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/turnitin-discloses-cyberattack-affecting-customer-facing-systems/

[TAM METİN - 443 kelime]
Educational technology company Turnitin disclosed that it detected unauthorized activity involving certain customer-facing systems and deployed its incident response plan.

In a public statement, CEO Chris Caren says that the incident is being investigated with help from external cybersecurity specialists, and law enforcement and cyber regulators have been notified.

Turnitin is a major education technology firm with a global presence, best known for its AI-generated writing detection, digital grading, academic research integrity checks, and examination proctoring.

According to its website, its software is used in over 16,000 institutions across 185 countries and serves 71 million students.

“We are writing to let you know that we recently identified unauthorized activity involving certain customer-facing Turnitin systems,” reads the letter from the CEO on the platform's website.

“Upon identifying the matter, we implemented our incident response plan, initiated an investigation with external cybersecurity specialists, and notified law enforcement and cyber regulators.”

Turnitin says its systems are fully operational and safe to use, noting that the affected systems do not collect government identification credentials or institutional financial information.

The company said its investigation into the incident remains ongoing and has not disclosed any details about the nature and scope of the unauthorized activity.

If you have any information regarding this incident or other undisclosed attacks, you can contact us confidentially via Signal at 646-961-3731 or at tips@bleepingcomputer.com.

Turnitin continues to monitor its systems for further unauthorized activity, indicating that it is still early to guarantee that the threat has been eliminated completely.

The company has not disclosed whether any user data was accessed or stolen, when the incident occurred, and how many individuals might have been impacted.

Regarding Turnitin service availability, the platform’s status page shows no incidents for November 2026, and the uptime history logs no disruptions. However, it's unclear whether it is safe to continue using the service and uploading sensitive data.

As of writing, no ransomware groups have publicly taken responsibility for the attack at Turnitin.

BleepingComputer has contacted the firm with questions about the incident, but a company representative said they have no additional details to share at this time.

[16] BleepingComputer - Radware discloses breach exposing customer cloud access keys
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Tue, 24 Nov 2026 12:49:05 -0500
Link: https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/radware-discloses-breach-exposing-customer-cloud-access-keys/

[TAM METİN - 484 kelime]
Cybersecurity company Radware says attackers gained access to some of its customers' account details and cloud access keys through an unsecured internal Cloud WAF (Web Application Firewall) management API that was left exposed online.

Founded in 1996 and headquartered in Tel Aviv, Radware now provides application delivery and DDoS protection services to more than 12,500 enterprise and telecom carrier customers worldwide.

In a root cause analysis report shared by an anonymous source with BleepingComputer, Radware says it blocked the originating IP address and access to the internal management API. The company also revoked and replaced all internal account credentials after detecting the breach on Thursday, ten hours after the initial unauthorized API access.

"On 19 November 2026, an unauthorized party accessed an internal-only Cloud WAF management API that was reachable from the Public Internet without requiring authentication," Radware said.

"The confirmed information potentially accessed includes customer contact details, and for a subset of customers, the credentials configured for log export into their self-managed storage (AWS S3, Azure Blob Storage, or SFTP)."

The company added that the breach did not expose WAF traffic inspection, certificates, end-users’ data, customer application traffic, or cloud DDoS services and data.

Customers advised to rotate exposed keys

It also "strongly" advised affected AppSec (WAF, BOT) customers to rotate Cloud WAF log export credentials (including Azure Blob Storage keys or SAS tokens, AWS S3 access keys, or SFTP credentials) as soon as possible and review access logs on their cloud storage accounts for unexpected access since at least November 19.

The company is still investigating the breach and whether any API keys or other customer information exposed in the incident were used without authorization, and said it has already received reports from customers about AWS alerts for an unfamiliar IP address attempting to access their log-export buckets.

"WAF traffic inspection and protection have continued without interruption, and we have not identified an impact on the availability or protection of customer applications and services," Radware added. "We have found no evidence to date of changes to customer security policies or of access to customer application traffic."

Radware has yet to issue a public statement regarding the incident or file a breach report with the US Securities and Exchange Commission.

BleepingComputer also reached out to a Radware spokesperson with questions about the incident, but a response was not immediately available.

[18] BleepingComputer - Police disrupt 57 illegal streaming services with 7.3 million visits
────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Tarih: Tue, 24 Nov 2026 10:10:10 -0500
Link: https://www.bleepingcomputer.com/news/security/police-disrupt-57-illegal-streaming-services-with-73-million-visits/

[TAM METİN - 507 kelime]
Law enforcement authorities from 11 countries have disrupted 57 illegal streaming services that operated hundreds of websites and drew over 7.3 million visits each year.

Such illegal IPTV services offer access to television channels, live sports, and movies without permission from the copyright owners for a small monthly fee.

According to the European Audiovisual Anti-Piracy Alliance (AAPA) trade association, legitimate service providers lose around €4.83 billion annually as roughly 60 million people in Europe use illegal platforms for streaming content.

"The operation resulted in the disruption of 57 illegal IPTV services and the identification of 321 websites illegally offering copyrighted content, with a combined annual traffic of more than 7.3 million visits. Investigators have so far traced over EUR 26 million in cryptocurrency transactions, with further analysis ongoing," Europol said in a Tuesday press release.

"The operation targeted a criminal market worth billions of euros annually, in which illegal streaming providers sell unauthorised access to premium television channels, live sports and films, often collecting subscription payments through cryptocurrency."

As part of this joint action, which also involved Europol, the European Union Intellectual Property Office (EUIPO), INTERPOL, and AAPA, the investigators also partnered with crypto industry experts from Binance, Coinbase, and Chainalysis to trace cryptocurrency payments and identify the financial networks powering these illegal IPTV services.

They did this by looking for links between the illegal IPTV services, crypto wallets, financial transactions, and the individuals who control these services, who used the wallets to receive and transfer payments, making it easier to move money between countries and hide the origin of their profits.

Europol and the European Union Intellectual Property Office (EUIPO) joined forces in 2016 to create the Intellectual Property Crime Coordinated Coalition (IPC3), which operates within Europol and targets counterfeiting and online and offline piracy.

In June, another major international operation coordinated by Bulgaria with Europol's support, dubbed "Operation KRATOS 2," resulted in the removal of more than 27,000 illegal streaming URLs, the shutdown of nine organized crime groups, and 29 arrests.

That followed Operation KRATOS, another international anti-piracy operation carried out in summer 2024, which shut down a massive illegal streaming network with over 22 million users worldwide.

Earlier this year, in January, Europol, Eurojust, and Interpol also coordinated Operation Switch Off, a global law enforcement action that led to the seizure of three industrial-scale illegal IPTV services.

More recently, in May, Italian authorities dismantled the CINEMAGOAL piracy platform, which provided illegal access to many online streaming platforms, including Netflix, Spotify, and Disney+.
